CP — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • CPKC moves loaded freight cars from origin to destination over about 20,000 route miles across Canada, the United States and Mexico, and it owns and operates the only freight railway spanning all three (10-K FY2025, Item 1).
  • CPKC reports one operating segment. The nine commodity lines below are a revenue disaggregation under ASC 606 with no allocated operating cost.
  • Three lines run through narrow channels anyway: all export potash is marketed by Canpotex, the Nutrien and Mosaic joint venture, or by K+S Potash Canada; almost all Canadian metallurgical coal originates at Elk Valley Resources mines in southeast British Columbia; and 46% of intermodal revenue is international containers whose routing ocean-carrier alliances set annually. Those three counterparties set volume on C$2,897m of FY2025 revenue.
  • The yield table is substantially a weight table. Revenue per ton-mile spans 7.2x across the nine lines, from potash at 3.32 cents to automotive at 23.85. Converted to revenue per gross ton-mile the same nine span 2.6x, from potash at 2.16 to metals and minerals at 5.73.
  • Management's story is near-shoring and the three-country land bridge, sized by Brooks at about C$100m of revenue in 2023, over C$600m in 2026 and a stated path to C$1bn, of which 65% moves between western Canada and Mexico (Q2 2026 earnings call, 2026-07-29). At C$600m that is 4.0% of FY2025 revenue and it grew about C$140m in 2025.
  • Master service agreements fix a price framework; the volume is re-won carload by carload. Four mechanisms set price, and management controls one of them.
  • Grain is 22% of freight revenue, Canadian grain is half of that, and 68% of Canadian grain is entitlement-regulated, which gives roughly 7% of freight revenue, about C$1.1bn
  • Roughly 40–55% of an average cost dollar recurs on the next ton-mile, which is why volume growth and volume decline both reach operating income with unusual force.
  • FY2025's 68% lands inside the 60–70% Velani guided at the 2023 Investor Day. H1 2026 at 40% does not.
competitive-advantage
  • CPKC owns an asset nobody can rebuild and earns a return on it that is below its cost of capital. Both statements describe the same company in 2026.
  • And returns and margins will reach the top of the industry: the 2024–2028 outlook, still on slide 22 of the September 2026 deck, targets high single-digit revenue growth, double-digit core adjusted EPS growth and a "return to double-digit Core adjusted ROIC".
  • BNSF and J.B. Hunt moved their Mexico intermodal service to Ferromex via Eagle Pass effective 1 January 2024, on a routing they state is a day faster
  • A six-network oligopoly with an absolute entry barrier and slack capacity should produce returns above the cost of capital for the networks with the densest traffic and the fewest competing routings. UNP's 15.0% return and 59.8% operating ratio are what that looks like. CPKC's franchise is longer-haul and thinner, its largest line is rate-capped, its Mexican leg touches one border crossing against Union Pacific's six, and its capital base includes the price of buying the southern half.
  • Operating ratio, CPKC minus the mean of the five peers, where negative means CPKC is better: −2.5 points (FY2019), −6.2 (FY2020), +0.7 (FY2021), +0.4 (FY2022), −1.2 (FY2023), +0.1 (FY2024), −1.1 (FY2025).
  • CPKC's own 0.9-point decline is arithmetic: fuel surcharge revenue rose C$258m to C$608m while fuel expense rose C$213m, and removing both from the calculation gives an operating margin of 41.7% in Q2 2026 and 41.7% in Q2 2025.
  • So the pricing claim holds on the measure management prices to and fails on the measure that scales with the work performed, and the difference is that CPKC is hauling roughly the same number of boxes further at a lower rate per ton-mile.
  • CPKC belongs in the middle band: an advantage that earns a return on the operating assets today and is defensible for a long time, but is open to erosion as rivals route around it, and one whose acquisition price has not been earned back.
  • The sharpest structural analogue is BNSF: an irreplaceable network bought at a large premium in 2010, whose balance sheet still holds US$15,351m of goodwill from that purchase, which earned 8.2% ROIC in FY2025 and 10.4% excluding the goodwill.
competitive-landscape
  • CPKC is the smallest of the six North American Class I railroads: sixth of six on revenue at US$10,770m equivalent, 11.1% of the six-carrier total; third on revenue ton-miles among the five that disclose them; and last on revenue per route mile.
  • It leads in three places: Canadian export potash, where it moves the large majority of Canpotex volume to Vancouver and, jointly with Union Pacific, all of it to Portland; Mexican automotive, where it reaches 90% of Mexican assembly plants
  • The Herfindahl-Hirschman index on these shares is 1,863. A Union Pacific–Norfolk Southern combination takes the merged share to 37.7% and the index to 2,493, a rise of 630; a BNSF–CSX response would take it to 3,190.
  • East of the Mississippi the structure is a CSX/NS duopoly, west of it UP/BNSF, in Canada CN/CPKC, and in Mexico CPKC against Grupo México's Ferromex and Ferrosur. CPKC is present in three of those four theatres.
  • CPKC earns 4.81 U.S. cents per revenue ton-mile, the lowest of the five carriers that disclose ton-miles, against CN 5.00, UNP 5.44, CSX 6.49 and NSC 6.62. That is mix and geography: CPKC's book is 36% bulk with long hauls and a statutory cap on the largest bulk line, against eastern merchandise books at CSX and NSC.
  • Under an agreement of July 2026, Union Pacific receives expanded operating rights over CN's former Elgin, Joliet & Eastern corridor around Chicago and CN gains operating rights over Union Pacific's network between Memphis and the Eagle Pass gateway to Ferromex
  • In crop year 2024-25 CN moved approximately 31 million tonnes of western Canadian grain, about 1 million above its previous record, while CPKC moved "in excess of 27 million tonnes", its highest since 2020-21 — roughly 53/47 in CN's favour
  • CPKC earns US$3,916 per automotive carload, 26% above the next-highest disclosed figure (NSC at US$3,121) and 28% above CN, because its ton-miles per carload are about 23,000 against CN's 15,300: finished vehicles moving from Mexican plants to Canada in a closed loop.
critical-points
  • Return on invested capital was 15.0% in FY2019 and 14.9% in FY2020, two to four points above the five-peer mean. It is 6.0% in FY2025, 5.2 points below that mean, last of six, and below the Surface Transportation Board's 10.68% determination of the industry cost of capital for 2024.
  • CN's coal revenue declined 5.3% in H1 2026 from the same southeast British Columbia basin against CPKC's 15.2%, so CPKC's single-origin exposure is three times the effect at its nearest comparable.
expectations
  • Consensus requires about 145 basis points of operating-ratio improvement a year for three consecutive years. Management's own stated pace is 100 basis points a year, and the first half of 2026 delivered 70 basis points of deterioration. No North American Class I has improved its operating ratio for four consecutive years starting from a sub-65% base.
  • Consensus of C$5.171 for CY2026 requires second-half core adjusted EPS of C$2.871 against C$2.438 a year earlier, +17.8%, and second-half revenue of C$8,524m, +12.4%, after a first half that grew 5.5% and 5.0%.
m-and-a
  • CP paid roughly 23x KCS's trailing EBITDA using stock and cash the market valued at roughly 17x, raised its own bid by US$25 per KCS share after Canadian National intervened, and spent a net US$700m on break fees to prevail.
  • Aggregate core adjusted income compounded at 13.3% a year from 2021 to 2025, matching legacy CP's pre-deal rate; adjusted diluted earnings per share compounded at 5.2% against 13.3% in the four years to 2021, and the eight-point difference went to the 34.3% increase in the diluted share count.
management-claims
  • Reaching 10% on an unchanged capital base requires core adjusted operating income near C$9.3bn, about 50% above FY2025, or roughly 100bp of improvement a year for three years against the 20bp added in 2025.
  • C$1.5bn of revenue at incremental EBITDA margins of 50–75% produces C$750m–1,125m, or US$545–815m, against the US$820m market-opportunity target plus US$180m of cost synergies, so the outcome reaches plan only at the top of that range
  • FY2025 GAAP operating ratios: UNP 59.8%, CN 61.9%, CPKC 62.8%, NSC 64.2%, BNSF 65.6%, CSX 67.9%. CPKC's core adjusted 59.9% would match UNP's GAAP figure, and the exclusion that produces it is KCS purchase-accounting amortization recurring over the acquired assets' useful lives — 250bp of the 290bp gap, every year, with a further 40bp of acquisition-related costs and a new C$27m of STB advisory costs in H1 2026.
  • The commitment was repeated for five years, was not reached, and was replaced with a measure CPKC does not report in its 10-K.
  • Consensus for CY2028 is C$6.837, 11% below the C$7.68 version and 13.5–14.5% below the C$7.90–8.00 version, and reaches the doubling only in 2029 on the C$7.800 CY2029 mean.
risks
  • Traffic CPKC hands to UP or NS at Chicago, Kansas City, Memphis or New Orleans becomes traffic a merged carrier can route end to end, and the pricing tension between two connections at a gateway collapses to one.
  • Exposure: C$2,711m of FY2025 revenue, 18.0%, and C$11,326m of properties and right-of-use assets, 20.3% of the total.
  • Cross-border traffic between the three countries is the merger thesis and is 41% of FY2025 freight revenue.
secular-trends
  • CPKC's freight demand is set at the origin. The nine lines divide into four mechanisms, and the two largest groups are supply-constrained.
  • At the June 2023 Investor Day CPKC set high single-digit revenue growth for 2024–2028, double-digit core adjusted EPS growth with "line of sight to… meaningfully double our EPS over that time frame", 3–4% pricing and revenue synergies of C$1.5bn-plus by year five.
  • Two years into a five-year plan, growth is running at roughly half the target rate, and the gap is not a pricing failure in the renewal book: it is that the base business did not grow and mix diluted realized yield.
  • Excluding grain, H1 2026 ton-miles declined 2.1%. Grain alone added 4,687m ton-miles at 5.19 cents, worth about C$243m of freight revenue against total underlying revenue growth of C$214m in the half. The record Canadian harvest accounts for more than all of the underlying revenue growth in the period.
  • On matched H1 2026 baskets, CPKC's ton-miles excluding grain, potash, fertilizers and coal grew 1.3% against CN's 1.7% excluding grain-and-fertilizers and coal, while total ton-miles grew 2.8% against 4.0%.
  • A step-up above C$300m against that means claimed synergies account for about 70% of all underlying growth, leaving the non-synergy base under 1%.
  • The Gemini Cooperation was the named driver of FY2025 international intermodal growth at Vancouver and Saint John, and in Q2 2026 those two ports were named as the source of the decline. Alliance routing is decided annually by third parties and reversed a line's growth in a single quarter.
technology
  • What separates CPKC from CN, UNP, NSC, CSX and BNSF is a route structure, a Mexican concession and a statutory pricing regime. None of those is a technology.
  • CPKC burned 18% more fuel per gross ton-mile than CN in FY2025 and 19% more in Q2 2026.
  • The train grows from 7,000 to 8,500 feet, new hopper cars three feet shorter hold 10–20% more grain each, and an 8,500-foot consist holds 147 cars instead of 112, raising loaded tonnage from 10,500 to 15,000 tonnes, an increase of 44% (2018 Investor Day, Joan Hardy).
  • Once an elevator is rebuilt to CPKC's loop-track geometry the switching cost to another railway is the cost of the elevator.
  • Alberta heavy crude is roughly 70% bitumen and 30% diluent by volume, and the diluent exists only to make the blend flow through a pipeline. Stripping it at origin produces a product that is non-hazardous for transport and lets a train hold about 30% more crude.
  • Cold wheel technology is the real advantage: wayside detectors at the base of descending grades measure the heat a brake shoe puts into each wheel, replacing a manual air-brake test that requires stopping the train and walking it.
  • FRA-reportable train accident frequency was 0.85 per million train-miles in FY2025 against CN's 1.84 and CSX's 3.08, and CPKC led all Class Is on that measure in 2023, 2024 and 2025.
  • MMX moves San Luis Potosí to Chicago in 3.9 days against 4.0 by single-driver truck and 6.8 by the next-closest interchange rail option, a 43% transit advantage over the rail alternative
value-chain
  • CPKC's 37.2% operating margin is the highest disclosed margin at any layer of this chain, above Wabtec's Freight operating margin of 19.5% and Mosaic's Potash gross margin of 32.7%.
  • Where the haul is a large fraction of what the shipper sells — grain at roughly 7% of canola and more of wheat — Parliament capped the price by formula. Where the haul is under 1% of what the shipper sells, automotive earned 26.43 cents per ton-mile in Q2 2026, 5.5x the coal yield of 4.82 cents in the same quarter, and no regulator intervenes.
  • Every disclosed capacity measure shows slack and it widened through Q2 2026: 292 locomotives in storage, 12.7% of the fleet; 82% freight-car in-service utilisation; FY2025 gross ton-miles up 4% on train miles up 1%.
  • Against the diesel index, neither CPKC nor its shippers set the price: the surcharge covered 86% of fuel expense in FY2025 when prices declined and 98% in Q2 2026 when On-Highway Diesel rose 52%, which is the opposite of what a simple lag would produce.

CPKC moves loaded freight cars from origin to destination over about 20,000 route miles across Canada, the United States and Mexico, and it owns and operates the only freight railway spanning all three (10-K FY2025, Item 1).

filingsupports ✦ best
Canadian Pacific Kansas City Limited ("CPKC" or the "Company") owns and operates the only freight railway spanning Canada, the United States ("U.S."), and Mexico. CPKC provides rail and intermodal transportation services over a network of approximately 20,000 miles, serving principal business centres across Canada, the U.S., and Mexico. CPKC transports bulk commodities, merchandise freight, and intermodal traffic.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingqualifies
The Company operates on a network of approximately 20,000 miles of main track, of which the Company accesses 3,300 miles under trackage rights. The Company's track network represents the size of the Company's operations that connects markets, customers, and other railways. The Company's network accesses the U.S. markets directly through five wholly-owned subsidiaries: Soo Line Railroad Company, a Class I railway operating in the U.S. Midwest; the Dakota, Minnesota & Eastern Railroad ("DM&E"), which operates in the U.S. Midwest; Delaware & Hudson Railway Company, Inc., which operates between eastern Canada and the U.S. Northeast; the Central Maine & Quebec Railway U.S. Inc., which operates in the U.S. Northeast, and the Kansas City Southern Railway Company, a Class I railway which operates in the central and south-central U.S.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
The Company's network in Canada covers approximately 8,400 miles and extends from the Port of Vancouver on Canada's Pacific Coast to the Port of Montreal and eastern Quebec and to the Port of Saint John via a haulage agreement. The U.S. network covers approximately 8,800 miles and extends through industrial centres of Chicago, Illinois; Detroit, Michigan; Buffalo and Albany, New York; Minneapolis, Minnesota; Kansas City, Missouri; and to the U.S. Gulf Coast with port access at Port Arthur, Texas, New Orleans, Louisiana, and Mobile, Alabama via agreement. The Company's network in Mexico extends approximately 3,100 miles from the Laredo, Texas border crossing through Mexico City, with port access at Lazaro Cardenas, Veracruz, Altamira, and Tampico.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingqualifies
CPKCM holds a concession from the Mexican government until June 2047, which is renewable under certain conditions, for additional periods, each up to 50 years (the "Concession"). CPKCM has the exclusive right to provide the freight rail service through 2037, subject to certain trackage and haulage rights granted to other freight rail concessionaires, and subject to trackage and haulage rights afforded to concessionaires of concessions that may be granted by the SICT to provide passenger rail service in the future.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26

The increased utilization of CPKC-served ports and strong service reliability is driving international intermodal growth to the CPKC network.

filingsupports ✦ best
The Company has master service agreements with customers which establish pricing, terms and conditions for future freight services the Company will provide when service requests or bills of lading are received from those customers. Each bill of lading or service request is a distinct performance obligation. Transaction prices are generally determined when bills of lading or service requests are initiated and are allocated to distinct performance obligations based on estimated standalone selling prices which are determined based on observable fair market values.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
Freight revenues are recognized over time as transportation services are provided and obligations under the terms of a contract with a customer are satisfied. ... Performance obligations not fully satisfied as at the balance sheet date are generally expected to be satisfied in the following reporting period. Contract liabilities represent payments received for performance obligations not yet satisfied. The short duration over which freight rail services are delivered generally means that there is an immaterial value of outstanding performance obligations and contract liabilities as at the balance sheet date.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
Fuel surcharge revenues are earned on individual shipments and are based primarily on the price of On-Highway Diesel in Canada and the U.S. and the public fuel price for Petroleos Mexicanos ("PEMEX") TAR Irapuato in Mexico. As such, fuel surcharge revenues are a function of freight volumes and fuel prices. Fuel surcharge revenues accounted for approximately 10% of the Company's Freight revenues in 2025. The Company is also subject to carbon taxation systems and levies in some jurisdictions in which it operates, the costs of which are passed on to the shipper.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
In 2026, the Company expects that every $0.01 weakening (or strengthening) of the Canadian dollar relative to the U.S. dollar, positively (or negatively) impacts Total revenues by approximately $78 million (2025 - approximately $76 million), negatively (or positively) impacts Operating expenses by approximately $45 million (2025 - approximately $43 million), and negatively (or positively) impacts Net interest expense by approximately $6 million (2025 - approximately $6 million) on an annualized basis.

Certain customer arrangements include variable consideration in the form of rebates, discounts, or incentives. Volume rebates are accrued based on estimated volumes and contract terms and recognized as a reduction of freight revenues as the related freight services are provided. Peer: Norfolk Southern Corporation (NSC)

filingsupports
A performance obligation is created when a customer under a transportation contract or public tariff submits a bill of lading to us for the transport of goods. These performance obligations are satisfied as the shipments move from origin to destination. As such, transportation revenues are recognized proportionally as a shipment moves, and related expenses are recognized as incurred. These performance obligations are generally short-term in nature with transit days averaging approximately one week or less for each commodity group.
— Norfolk Southern Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-09

filingsupports
The majority of Canadian grain shipments are regulated by the Canadian government through the Canada Transportation Act (the "CTA"). This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement ("MRE"). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC Annual Report on Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26

Peer: Union Pacific Corporation

filingsupports
Union Pacific Railroad Company connects 23 states in the western two-thirds of the country by rail ... operates from all major West Coast and Gulf Coast ports to Eastern gateways, connects with Canada's rail systems, and is the only railroad serving all six major Mexico gateways.
— Union Pacific Corporation Form 10-K FY2025, Item 1 Business, 2026-02-06

A six-network oligopoly with an absolute entry barrier and slack capacity should produce returns above the cost of capital for the networks with the densest traffic and the fewest competing routings. UNP's 15.0% return and 59.8% operating ratio are what that looks like. CPKC's franchise is longer-haul and thinner, its largest line is rate-capped, its Mexican leg touches one border crossing against Union Pacific's six, and its capital base includes the price of buying the southern half.

filingsupports
Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC Form 10-K FY2025, Item 1. Business - Grain, 2026-02-26

filingsupports
The Company may freely set rates on a non-discriminatory basis up to the maximum rates registered with the ATTRAPI. ... If the CNA determines that there is no effective competition for particular movements, the ATTRAPI could set rates for those movements or grant limited trackage rights to another railroad while the condition of no effective competition remains.
— CPKC Form 10-K FY2025, Item 1. Business - Government Regulation, Mexico, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
CPKCM holds a concession from the Mexican government until June 2047, which is renewable under certain conditions, for additional periods, each up to 50 years (the "Concession"). CPKCM has the exclusive right to provide the freight rail service through 2037 ... During the remainder of the Concession period, CPKCM is required to pay the Mexican government an annual concession duty equal to 1.25% of gross revenues.
— CPKC Form 10-K FY2025, Item 1. Business - Government Regulation, Mexico, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
The CTA establishes a common carrier obligation and it indirectly regulates rates by providing shippers access to regulatory mechanisms for challenging freight rates, including ancillary charges, and access to regulated interswitching rates and long-haul interswitching rates, and regulatory mechanisms to challenge level of service.

This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement ("MRE"). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26

CPKCM holds a concession from the Mexican government until June 2047, which is renewable under certain conditions, for additional periods, each up to 50 years (the "Concession"). CPKCM has the exclusive right to provide the freight rail service through 2037, subject to certain trackage and haulage rights granted to other freight rail concessionaires ... websupports
The Surface Transportation Board (STB) has determined that the cost of capital for the railroad industry, which is calculated each year, was 10.68% for 2024. The cost of capital represents the STB Office of Economics' estimate of the average rate of return needed to persuade investors to provide capital to the freight rail industry, according to the STB's July 20, 2025, decision. The cost of capital was 9.87% for 2023, 10.58% in 2022, 10.37% in 2021, and 7.89% in 2020.
— Marybeth Luczak, Executive Editor, Railway Age, 'Railroad Cost of Capital Set at 10.68% for 2024' (reporting STB Docket EP 558 Sub-No. 28), 2025-07-22

So we will be targeting some of that business, certainly. We are at par on transit for Western Canada."
— Tracy Robinson, President & CEO, CN, CN eyes Mexico growth thanks to haulage rights deal with Union Pacific (Trains), 2026-09-16

The majority of Canadian grain shipments are regulated by the Canadian government through the Canada Transportation Act (the "CTA"). This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement ("MRE"). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments.
— CPKC 2025 Annual Report (Form 10-K), Item 1 Business, Grain, 2026-02-26

KCS purchase accounting represents the amortization of basis differences being the incremental depreciation and amortization in relation to fair value adjustments to properties and intangible assets ... All assets subject to KCS purchase accounting contribute to income generation and will continue to amortize over their estimated useful lives. Excluding KCS purchase accounting from GAAP results provides financial statement users with additional transparency by isolating the impact of KCS purchase accounting.

filingqualifies
CPKCM holds a concession from the Mexican government until June 2047, which is renewable under certain conditions, for additional periods, each up to 50 years (the "Concession"). CPKCM has the exclusive right to provide the freight rail service through 2037, subject to certain trackage and haulage rights granted to other freight rail concessionaires... CPKCM has the right to use, but does not own, all track and buildings that are necessary for the rail lines' operation. CPKCM is obligated to maintain the right of way, track structure, buildings and related maintenance facilities to the operational standards specified in the Concession agreement and to return the assets in that condition at the end of the Concession period. During the remainder of the Concession period, CPKCM is required to pay the Mexican government an annual concession duty equal to 1.25% of gross revenues.
— CPKC Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
If the Concession is terminated or revoked by the SICT for any reason, CPKCM would receive no compensation and its interest in its rail lines, and all other fixtures covered by the Concession, as well as all improvements made by it, would revert to the Mexican government. Revocation or termination of the Concession could have a material adverse effect on the Company's results of operations.
— CPKC Form 10-K for fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
The Company's network in Canada covers approximately 8,400 miles and extends from the Port of Vancouver on Canada's Pacific Coast to the Port of Montréal and eastern Québec and to the Port of Saint John via a haulage agreement. The U.S. network covers approximately 8,800 miles and extends through industrial centres of Chicago, Illinois; Detroit, Michigan; Buffalo and Albany, New York; Minneapolis, Minnesota; Kansas City, Missouri; and to the U.S. Gulf Coast with port access at Port Arthur, Texas, New Orleans, Louisiana, and Mobile, Alabama via agreement. A decline or disruption in domestic, cross-border, or global economic conditions, including fluctuations in interest rates and changes to international trade policies and tariffs, that affect the supply or demand for commodities that the Company transports may decrease the Company's freight volumes. filingsupports
The majority of Canadian grain shipments are regulated by the Canadian government through the Canada Transportation Act (the “CTA”). This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement (“MRE”). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
The Company’s Potash traffic primarily moves from Saskatchewan to offshore markets through the Ports of Vancouver, Portland, and Thunder Bay, as well as to domestic markets in the U.S. Midwest. Potash shipments for export beyond Canada and the U.S. are marketed by Canpotex Limited (“Canpotex”) or K+S Potash Canada. Canpotex is an export company jointly-owned by Nutrien Ltd. and The Mosaic Company.
— CPKC Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26
filingsupports
In Canada, the Company transports mostly metallurgical coal destined for export for use in the steelmaking process. The Company’s Canadian coal traffic originates mainly from Elk Valley Resources' mines in the southeast region of British Columbia (“B.C.”). The Company primarily moves coal west from the mines destined to port terminals for export to world markets (Pacific Rim, Europe, India, and South America).
— CPKC Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025, 2026-02-26

RTMs is a measure of volumes and is calculated by multiplying the weight in tons of the shipment lading being transported by the number of miles that the shipment is transported on company lines. CN uses RTMs as the primary measure of volumes as compared to carloads, since RTMs also takes into account the length of haul and weight in the movement.

filingsupports ✦ best
Canadian Pacific Kansas City Limited ("CPKC" or the "Company") owns and operates the only freight railway spanning Canada, the United States ("U.S."), and Mexico. CPKC provides rail and intermodal transportation services over a network of approximately 20,000 miles, serving principal business centres across Canada, the U.S., and Mexico. CPKC transports bulk commodities, merchandise freight, and intermodal traffic.
— CPKC FY2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, Item 1. Business, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
CPKCM holds a concession from the Mexican government until June 2047, which is renewable under certain conditions, for additional periods, each up to 50 years (the "Concession"). CPKCM has the exclusive right to provide the freight rail service through 2037, subject to certain trackage and haulage rights granted to other freight rail concessionaires... CPKCM has the right to use, but does not own, all track and buildings that are necessary for the rail lines' operation... During the remainder of the Concession period, CPKCM is required to pay the Mexican government an annual concession duty equal to 1.25% of gross revenues.
— CPKC FY2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, Item 1. Business, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
The majority of Canadian grain shipments are regulated by the Canadian government through the Canada Transportation Act (the "CTA"). This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement ("MRE"). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC FY2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, Item 1. Business, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
The Company is in the ground transportation and logistics business and competes with other railways, motor carriers, ship and barge operators, and pipelines. Depending on the specific market, competing railways, motor carriers, and other competitors may exert pressure on price and service levels. The Company continually evaluates the market needs and the competition. The Company responds as it deems appropriate to provide competitive services to the market. This includes developing new offerings such as transload facilities, new train services, and other logistics services.
— CPKC FY2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, Item 1. Business, 2026-02-12
presentationsupports
As you may have heard 8 days ago, we had an exciting announcement with a partnership with CSX. Partnering with the Class I railroad is a pivotal point in this project. It's going to allow us to do a few things. One is with a partner like CSX, we will gain their understanding and help on the conversion side with the facilities they have in West Virginia. We bring our technology to them. And working together, we will accelerate the number of assets that go into service.

Transport Canada has adopted the use of ATIP technology within its updated rules related to railway track safety and inspection methods in Canada.

The train grows from 7,000 to 8,500 feet, new hopper cars three feet shorter hold 10–20% more grain each, and an 8,500-foot consist holds 147 cars instead of 112, raising loaded tonnage from 10,500 to 15,000 tonnes, an increase of 44% (2018 Investor Day, Joan Hardy).

filingqualifies
The Company's network is unique among railways in North America as it is strategically positioned in the heart of grain-producing regions of western Canada and the northern plains of the U.S. The Company also provides a service advantage, by way of its 8,500-foot High Efficiency Product ("HEP") Trains, including high-capacity hopper cars, which enables the Company to efficiently serve farmers, shippers, and the entire grain supply chain. The 8,500-foot HEP Trains can move approximately 40% more grain than the prior generation of grain trains.
— CPKC FY2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, Item 7 MD&A, Grain, 2026-02-12

And the cars are shorter so they're 3 feet shorter than the cars that they're replacing. So in our standard historical 7,000-foot train, instead of carrying 112 cars, we'll be able to carry 118 cars. But really, the exciting part is when you layer on the 8,500-foot high-efficiency product and those new cars... So the 8,500-foot train length with the shorter cars will carry 147 hopper cars. The third thing is, that during that operation, the train remains completely intact. The train is not separated, and that's important for our operation in terms of cold weather... keeping the train line together means that the pressure on the air brake system is intact at all times. As soon as a train is separated and the brake line is separated, the pressure is lost in the air brake system. And then, putting the train back together and repressurizing can be very challenging in cold winters, cold weather. So this means that we avoid any delays associated with the air brake.

Alberta heavy crude is roughly 70% bitumen and 30% diluent by volume, and the diluent exists only to make the blend flow through a pipeline. Stripping it at origin produces a product that is non-hazardous for transport and lets a train hold about 30% more crude.

Right now, we run 3 typical crude trains a day, which is -- for those of you that don't understand, it's 70% bitumen and 30% diluent, so that it's -- it has enough viscosity to move through the pipeline to get into the railcar. Cold wheel technology is the real advantage: wayside detectors at the base of descending grades measure the heat a brake shoe puts into each wheel, replacing a manual air-brake test that requires stopping the train and walking it.

competitive-position · technology · ↩ back to dossier

Company

earnings callsupports ✦ best
Cold wheel technology is another space that we pioneered in the industry. We created a technology using our wayside equipment detection to measure the heat imprint that the brakes are applying to the wheels. So as opposed to doing a break leakage test, we've got a technology that applies that in the movement of the train, we know how each individual car is breaking. We know where we might have a potential problem. To be able to apply that technology to drive a more reliable fleet is what we proved with the science and with the technology in our coal fleet. Now we've been allowed an application and exemption to apply that to the potash fleet.

So not just doing an initial terminal 100-year-old process, where we make sure that the brake supply and release on every car before a lease a terminal, but literally in the process of a train moving down the railway, you put the coal wheel detector at the location as it descends a hill, a grade. If the brakes are working effectively, each of those wheels should be a certain temperature. Well, if you have a wheel that's a meaningful variable less, then you know, wait a second. Something is not working properly with that break valve or with that braking system that the digital eye can't see.
— Keith E. Creel, CEO, President & Director, CPKC presents at J.P. Morgan 2023 Industrials Conference, 2023-03-16
presentationsupports
But it's not just hydrogen technology, cold wheel technology, we're pioneers in the industry. Everybody talks about hot box. Hot box is measuring the temperature of the journal, the bearing that the wheel turns on. Cold wheel technology is using that same technology but applying algorithms and measuring the heat of the wheel with wheel heat detectors, which all of our hot boxes are equipped also with dragging equipment and wheel heat detectors.
— Keith E. Creel, CEO, President & Director, CPKC presents at J.P. Morgan 2023 Industrials Conference, 2023-03-16
presentationsupports
So the next time it goes by a rep track, let's set the car out and let's get it on the [road] track. Let's do an electronic valve brake test, which is the most robust way to test a train brake, and what you have is a more robust train break system. So we took that technology and initially applied it to the cold trains. And we got a waiver from Transport Canada to waive safety inspections to use this technology. We've now since taken that and applied it to our potash fleet and now built the algorithms to apply it to the grain fleet.
— Keith E. Creel, CEO, President & Director, CPKC presents at J.P. Morgan 2023 Industrials Conference, 2023-03-16
presentationsupports
One last example, excuse me, I lost my train of time, our cold wheel technology, using our wayside detector systems, we were able to use data as it flows into the back office, and we're able to check the brake effectiveness and exactly how our trains are performing from that standpoint.
— Justin Meyer, Senior VP Engineering and Mechanical, CPKC 2023 Analyst/Investor Day, 2023-06-28
presentationqualifies
We're running bigger trains. It means less tolerance, trains break [indiscernible]. You got to walk the trains 2 miles long in the middle of the snow, that's a long way to walk back and change a knuckle, one man or woman. It's a tall task.
— Keith E. Creel, CEO, President & Director, CPKC presents at Citi's 2025 Global Industrial Tech and Mobility Conference, 2025-02-18
presentationqualifies
Yes. I think we've made some progress. It's taken a little bit more time than maybe any of us had wanted or anticipated, but we're starting to make the right moves. The autonomous track inspection that finally, that got passed through, we're able to start to look at how we justify and invest in expanding that technology across our networks. There's some unique things that we've done in Canada that we pioneered relative to cold-wheel technology, train inspections, eliminating regulatory inspections, extend the length of haul that -- it's all about asset turns, improving safety and improving efficiency. We've got an administration now that's entertaining those changes.
— Keith E. Creel, CEO, President & Director, CPKC presents at Citi's Global Industrial Tech & Mobility Conference 2026, 2026-02-18
presentationqualifies
So the things that we're trying to do, we've done some very innovative things in Canada where we've eliminated regulatory inspections through the use of our portals, the use of cold technology to test the braking systems on the trains. We're trying to bring that to the United States. We're working closely with the FRA. We're working to try to get a pilot to do that, which I think is going to give us a stepped improvement in productivity, efficiency and safety. We're not there yet. It's -- they're slower than I'd like them to be, but we do have a very good relationship, and we're going to continue to work on that.

filingsupports ✦ best
The majority of Canadian grain shipments are regulated by the Canadian government through the Canada Transportation Act (the "CTA"). This regulated business is subject to a maximum revenue entitlement ("MRE"). Under the CTA, railways can set their own rates for individual movements. However, the MRE governs aggregate revenues earned by the railway based on a formula that factors in the total volume, length of haul, average revenue per ton, and inflationary adjustments. The regulation applies to western Canadian export grain shipments to the ports of Vancouver and Thunder Bay.
— CPKC 2025 Annual Report on Form 10-K, 2026-02-26

The Company’s rail operations in Canada are subject to economic regulation by the Canadian Transportation Agency (the "Agency") pursuant to authorities under the CTA. The CTA establishes a common carrier obligation and it indirectly regulates rates by providing shippers access to regulatory mechanisms for challenging freight rates, including ancillary charges, and access to regulated interswitching rates and long-haul interswitching rates, and regulatory mechanisms to challenge level of service.
